- **Tags**: `thermodynamics`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `second law`  `closed quantum systems`  `many-body physics`

### TL;DR

A theoretical framework shows how the second law of thermodynamics emerges in closed quantum many-body systems from purely unitary quantum dynamics.

### Motivation

There is a long-standing tension between the reversible dynamics of closed quantum systems and the irreversible second law of thermodynamics; this paper aims to resolve it by deriving macroscopic thermodynamic behavior from pure quantum states.

### Method

The authors develop a theoretical framework for closed quantum many-body systems, analyzing how thermodynamic irreversibility and macroscopic laws emerge from quantum mechanical evolution without invoking external randomness or collapse.

### Result

They demonstrate that macroscopic thermodynamic principles, including the second law, can emerge naturally even when the underlying quantum state remains pure and evolves unitarily.

### Conclusion

The work reconciles quantum mechanics with thermodynamics in isolated many-body systems and clarifies the emergence of macroscopic irreversibility from quantum dynamics.

*Keywords: quantum thermodynamics, second law, closed quantum systems, many-body physics, irreversibility*

### TL;DR

The paper introduces post-selection games, a generalization of nonlocal games with discarded rounds, and develops algorithms for their local and Tsirelson bounds.

### Motivation

Standard nonlocal games do not naturally capture possibilistic nonlocality proofs such as Hardy's paradox or Bell tests with low detection efficiency.

### Method

The authors define post-selection games in which each round can be won, lost, or discarded, formalize possibilistic nonlocality, and develop algorithms to compute local and Tsirelson bounds.

### Result

They show that post-selection games can have an unbounded statistical-power advantage over traditional nonlocal games and are well suited to analyzing Bell tests with low detection efficiency.

### Conclusion

Post-selection games provide a useful theoretical framework for nonlocality and low-efficiency Bell tests, extending standard nonlocal-game methods.

*Keywords: nonlocal games, Bell nonlocality, Tsirelson bounds, post-selection, Hardy's paradox*

### Motivation

### Method

The authors define a QAKE protocol and analyze its security using an adapted classical authentication key exchange framework with separate conditions for authentication and data privacy. ## 17. The Three Kinds Of Three-qubit Entanglement

- **Score**: 8.1 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes

- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-10-22
- **Authors**: Szilárd Szalay
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-22-2242/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-10-22-2242/)
- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `three-qubit entanglement`  `W-state entanglement`  `three-tangle`  `concurrence`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces a polynomial measure of W-entanglement for pure three-qubit states, completing the trio of polynomial entanglement measures alongside the three-tangle (GHZ) and concurrence (bipartite), and proves a strict ordering among them.

### Motivation

Entanglement theory for pure three-qubit states lacked a polynomial measure quantifying W-type entanglement, even though GHZ-type entanglement and bipartite entanglement already had well-established polynomial measures (three-tangle and concurrence).

### Method

A polynomial invariant/measure of W-entanglement is constructed in parallel to the three-tangle and concurrence constructions, and its ordering relative to these existing measures is proven analytically.

### Result

The new W-entanglement measure is shown to be ordered between the bipartite concurrence and the GHZ three-tangle, mirroring the ordering of the corresponding three-qubit entanglement classes (bipartite < W < GHZ).

### Conclusion

The three polynomial measures and their ordered hierarchy justify treating the three kinds of three-qubit entanglement as genuinely distinct and systematically ordered, filling an important gap in multipartite entanglement theory.

*Keywords: three-qubit entanglement, W-state entanglement, three-tangle, concurrence, entanglement measures, SLOCC classes*

### Method

The paper constructs an algebraic representation of the Lindblad superoperator for finite-dimensional open quantum systems and benchmarks it against direct and Kronecker implementations.

### Motivation

Open quantum system dynamics are typically represented via Lindblad master equations, but explicit algebraic structure and efficient computational representations for multiqubit systems remain challenging.

### Method

### Result

The algebraic implementation reduces peak memory and construction time relative to a direct method, but standard Kronecker methods outperform both alternatives and scale to larger qubit numbers; examples confirm consistency and show leakage and positivity checks.

### Motivation

Topological orders are candidate quantum memories, so it is crucial to know how robustly the quantum information they store survives realistic local decoherence such as density/dephasing noise.

### Method

The authors map the decoherence dynamics of fractional quantum Hall states (Laughlin and Moore-Read) under density dephasing onto statistical-mechanics models, extracting the lifetime/robustness of the encoded anyonic information as a function of filling factor.

### Result

Moore-Read (non-Abelian) states retain their encoded information under arbitrary density dephasing, whereas Laughlin states exhibit infinite robustness only above a critical filling factor.

### Conclusion

Non-Abelian fractional quantum Hall states offer intrinsically decoherence-resilient quantum information storage, sharpening the case for topological anyonic memories while delineating regimes of failure for Abelian states.

Extensive numerical simulations are used to study how active dissipation alters the conservative Lorentz gas phase space, supported by an energy-variance filtering method that classifies periodic, quasi-ballistic, and chaotic trajectories and enables bifurcation diagrams in a non-conservative setting.

### Result

The authors find that stability islands collapse into attracting and repelling sets, and identify parameter regimes with strong bifurcation cascades, anomalous diffusion, and significant phase-space contraction.

### Conclusion

The active Lorentz gas provides a framework for studying transport in dissipative dynamical systems and bridges active matter with classical chaotic transport, with possible relevance to hydrodynamic quantum analogs in periodic media.

*Keywords: active Lorentz gas, walking droplets, anomalous diffusion, deterministic chaos, dissipative dynamical systems*

### Result

### Motivation

Gapless symmetry-protected topological phases and symmetry-enriched critical points go beyond the Landau-Ginzburg-Wilson paradigm, but whether the paradigmatic tricritical Ising CFT admits a topologically nontrivial realization with distinct boundary physics was unclear.

### Method

### Result

The original model realizes the free conformal boundary condition while the cluster model realizes a spontaneously fixed one, distinguished by different time-reversal charges of the disorder field despite identical conventional BCFT data; the splitting decays exponentially rather than algebraically, and the topological TCI persists along a critical line.

### Conclusion

A symmetry-enriched TCI universality class is established, revealing boundary distinctions invisible to conventional boundary CFT data and highlighting the role of symmetry enrichment and boundary RG flow.

- **Tags**: `acoustic topological insulator`  `coupled-mode theory`  `Bloch oscillation`  `pseudospin states`  `topological heterostructure`

### TL;DR

A theoretical and experimental study of coupled propagation between two pseudospin acoustic topological states in a topological heterostructure, mapped onto a two-waveguide coupled-mode model exhibiting Bloch oscillations and wavelength-selective demultiplexing.

### Motivation

Topological acoustic states offer robust energy transport, but quantitative control over the coupling between multiple topological channels remains underexplored, limiting multifunctional device design.

### Method

The heterostructure is modeled as two coupled waveguides within coupled-mode theory, with propagation constants and inter-state coupling strength extracted from supercell projected band structures; predictions are verified experimentally in an acoustic platform.

### Result

Decoupled states confine energy to the excited interface, while coupled states produce Bloch oscillations between two topological interfaces whose energy-transfer coefficient is tunable via interface detuning; dispersion of the coupling strength enables demultiplexing.

### Conclusion

The work provides a generalized coupled-mode framework for analyzing and manipulating topological-state coupling, enabling topologically protected multichannel acoustic devices.

*Keywords: acoustic topological insulator, coupled-mode theory, Bloch oscillation, pseudospin states, topological heterostructure*

### TL;DR

Angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy of single-layer nickelates reveals Luttinger liquid behavior arising from a squarelike Fermi surface.

### Motivation

Nickelates are close analogs of cuprates, and understanding their low-energy electronic excitations could clarify unconventional superconductivity and non-Fermi-liquid physics in correlated oxides.

### Method

The authors perform high-resolution ARPES measurements on single-layer nickelate samples and analyze quasiparticle line shapes, dispersions, and temperature dependence of spectral features.

### Result

The spectra show power-law line shapes and momentum-dependent scattering consistent with Luttinger liquid behavior, attributed to the squarelike Fermi surface topology.

### Conclusion

Single-layer nickelates can host Luttinger liquid physics, extending non-Fermi-liquid phenomenology beyond cuprates and motivating further study of correlation effects in nickelate platforms.

*Keywords: Luttinger liquid, nickelates, ARPES, Fermi surface, strongly correlated electrons*

- **Tags**: `single-particle tracking`  `ergodicity breaking`  `mean-squared increment`  `anomalous diffusion`  `stochastic processes`

### TL;DR

The paper introduces the mean-squared increment as a new statistic for single-particle tracking time series, allowing sharper and unambiguous discrimination between ergodic and non-ergodic (including spurious non-ergodicity) stochastic processes.

### Motivation

Standard ergodicity tests based on time-averaged mean-squared displacement are ambiguous and can misclassify processes, especially for finite trajectories and anomalous diffusion, so a more reliable diagnostic is needed.

### Method

The authors define and analytically characterize the mean-squared increment of positional time series and compare its behavior across classes of stochastic processes, including cases exhibiting genuine versus apparent (spurious) non-ergodicity.

### Result

The mean-squared increment is shown to distinguish ergodic from non-ergodic behaviour more precisely than previous estimators and to separate different classes of anomalous stochastic processes.

### Conclusion

The proposed statistic provides a practical and unambiguous tool for ergodicity assessment in single-particle tracking experiments, clarifying the interpretation of apparent non-ergodicity in finite data.

*Keywords: single-particle tracking, ergodicity breaking, mean-squared increment, anomalous diffusion, stochastic processes*

## 304. Conductivity Of A non-Galilean-invariant Fermi Liquid: Exact Solution Of The Kinetic Equation

- **Source**: Phys. Rev. B  |  **Feed**: Physical Review B

- **Authors**: Tatia Kiliptari, Vladimir I. Yudson, And Dmitrii L. Maslov
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/d791-lz1h](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/d791-lz1h)
- **Tags**: `fermi-liquid`  `Fermi liquid`  `kinetic equation`  `Planckian bound`  `electron-electron scattering`

### TL;DR

The paper exactly solves the kinetic equation for dc conductivity in a disordered, non-Galilean-invariant Fermi liquid, clarifying how electron-electron collisions affect current relaxation and the Planckian bound near a quantum critical point.

### Motivation

A apparent paradox exists because quasiparticle scattering scales as T^2 while the low-temperature resistivity correction can scale as T^2, suggesting strongly suppressed current relaxation; the authors aim to resolve this by treating electron-electron collisions consistently in transport.

### Method

They solve the linearized kinetic equation exactly for a disordered Fermi liquid without Galilean invariance, accounting for momentum-conserving electron-electron collisions and impurity scattering.

### Result

Electron-electron collisions enter conductivity via the quasiparticle scattering time, which governs the crossover between collisionless and hydrodynamic transport; the extra T^2 factor arises from Fermi-surface momentum conservation, and the electron-electron contribution remains parametrically small in the Fermi-liquid regime.

### Conclusion

Extrapolating to a T=3 quantum critical point, the renormalized quasiparticle scattering rate saturates the Planckian bound, but the work is a condensed-matter transport study not directly connected to the specified quantum information research directions.

*Keywords: Fermi liquid, kinetic equation, Planckian bound, electron-electron scattering, quantum critical point*

- **Score**: 1.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. Res.  |  **Feed**: Physical Review Research
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-11-04
- **Authors**: J. J. Briguglio, J. Lee, A. K. Lee, V. Hakim, And S. Romani
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/hvk8-9hl2](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/hvk8-9hl2)
- **Tags**: `place cells`  `power-law scaling`  `criticality`  `hippocampal activity`  `renormalization group`

### TL;DR

The paper shows that power-law scaling in coarse-grained hippocampal neural activity during spatial navigation arises from heterogeneity in place-cell properties rather than from genuine critical dynamics.

### Motivation

Power-law scaling in coarse-grained neural data is often interpreted as evidence of criticality, but the actual generative mechanism behind such scaling is frequently ambiguous, motivating a mechanistic re-examination of hippocampal recordings.

### Method

The authors analyze recorded neural activity during spatial navigation, reproducing power-law scaling via a phenomenological renormalization group (PRG) procedure and then testing models that attribute the scaling to place-cell heterogeneity.

### Result

The observed power-law exponents can be reproduced by accounting for heterogeneity across place cells, indicating that the scaling need not originate from an underlying critical point.

### Conclusion

Coarse-grained power laws in hippocampal activity should not be taken as automatic evidence of criticality; cell-level heterogeneity is a sufficient and parsimonious explanation.

*Keywords: place cells, power-law scaling, criticality, hippocampal activity, renormalization group*

Agner, Urs Hollenstein, Andreas Osterwalder, Hansjürg Schmutz, Daniel Sprecher, Andrea Wüest, Wim Ubachs, Christian Jungen, Kjeld S. E. Eikema, And Frédéric Merkt

- **Tags**: `molecular hydrogen`  `ionization energy`  `dissociation energy`  `precision spectroscopy`  `quantum electrodynamics tests`

### TL;DR

Precision measurements of the ionization and dissociation energies of molecular hydrogen improve the accuracy of these benchmark quantities fivefold, providing stringent tests of two-electron quantum-electrodynamics calculations and constraints on physics beyond the Standard Model.

### Motivation

H2 and its isotopologues are the simplest neutral molecules and thus serve as benchmark systems where extremely accurate ab initio quantum calculations can be compared with experiment, enabling tests of fundamental physics such as searches for extra forces or variation of fundamental constants.

### Method

High-resolution spectroscopic measurements (including advanced laser/maser techniques in the groups involved) determine the ionization and dissociation energies of molecular hydrogen with substantially reduced uncertainty, compared against state-of-the-art two-electron relativistic QED calculations.

### Result

The ionization and dissociation energies of H2 are determined with a fivefold improvement in accuracy, establishing new benchmark values for two-electron quantum calculations and tightening constraints on beyond-Standard-Model physics.

### Conclusion

The improved benchmark energies make molecular hydrogen a more precise testing ground for fundamental physics, though the work is a precision metrology and molecular-physics study without direct connection to quantum information, thermodynamics, networks, or hybrid quantum systems.

*Keywords: molecular hydrogen, ionization energy, dissociation energy, precision spectroscopy, quantum electrodynamics tests*

### Motivation

X-ray absorption spectroscopy is important for understanding battery cathode materials, but extracting oxidation-state information from experimental spectra requires expensive classical simulations.

The authors optimize a time-domain XAS simulation algorithm using compressed double-factorized Hamiltonians, product formulas, eigenvalue-error Trotter bounds, and a Lorentzian-tail-aware sampling strategy, then validate the reconstructed spectrum on a simulator against a classical reference.

### TL;DR

Extensive molecular simulations quantify how the rapid vitrification required for cryo-EM perturbs biomolecular conformational ensembles away from equilibrium and how those populations recover.

### Motivation

Cryo-EM traps biomolecules in vitreous ice via very fast cooling, but whether and how much this quench distorts the underlying equilibrium structural ensemble is unknown, which complicates interpretation of cryo-EM density maps.

### Method

Large-scale molecular dynamics simulations of biomolecules subject to fast and slow cooling protocols, characterizing the vitrification process and tracking the subsequent relaxation of conformational populations.

### Result

The authors quantify the degree of non-equilibrium perturbation induced by fast cooling and characterize the timescales over which equilibrium populations are recovered after vitrification.

### Conclusion

Vitrification can measurably bias observed structural ensembles in cryo-EM, and understanding the recovery of equilibrium populations is needed to correctly interpret cryo-EM data.

### Motivation

Global nuclear models must predict properties of exotic nuclei near drip lines, superheavy elements, and r-process pathways where no data exist, and standard constant-error assumptions misrepresent extrapolation uncertainty.

### Method

### Result

The heteroscedastic approach yields more realistic, position-dependent uncertainty quantification for predicted nuclear observables than constant-error Bayesian combination, particularly in regions far from measured nuclei.

### TL;DR

A long-needle atomic force microscopy platform is used to mechanotype individual liquid-liquid phase-separated condensates, distinguishing capillary-dominated from bulk-viscoelastic mechanical regimes.

### Motivation

The standard capillary-fluid picture of condensate mechanics may fail for biomolecular condensates containing transient multivalent molecular networks, so a model-independent way to determine the dominant mechanical regime is needed.

### Method

Single microscale droplets are probed with a vertically aligned glass fiber (long-needle AFM), decomposing forces into interfacial contact-line, dynamic-meniscus dissipation, and bulk resistance without assuming Newtonian behavior or a constitutive model.

### Result

Electrostatic polypeptide coacervates are capillary-dominated with ion-tunable interfacial tension and shear thinning, whereas protein condensates such as Ataxin-2 are bulk-viscoelastic, showing rate-dependent apparent modulus and exponential-then-power-law relaxation.

### Conclusion

The work provides a quantitative framework for identifying the dominant mechanical regime of LLPS condensates and a general strategy for microscale mechanics of complex fluids.

### TL;DR

A plasma-physics study of how a finite normal magnetic field modifies the Harris current-sheet equilibrium and destroys classical resistive tearing on the tearing timescale.

### Motivation

Classical resistive tearing theory presumes a fixed equilibrium current sheet, an assumption that fails when a normal magnetic field evolves the background as fast as the tearing mode grows.

### Method

Analytical treatment of a transversely pressure-balanced Harris profile with initial half-thickness and asymptotic reconnecting field, examining the coupled evolution of the background equilibrium and the tearing instability.

### Result

The normal field drives background evolution on the tearing timescale, so the standard prescribed-equilibrium tearing picture breaks down and modified growth behaviour results.

### Conclusion

Tearing in current sheets with a finite normal field requires a time-dependent equilibrium treatment rather than the classical static Harris-sheet analysis.

*Keywords: magnetic reconnection, current sheet, resistive tearing mode, Harris equilibrium, plasma astrophysics*

### TL;DR

### Motivation

Senescent cells are normally cleared by immune cells but accumulate in tissues during aging and age-related disease; the reasons for this accumulation and the role of senescent immune cells themselves were unclear.

### Method

### Result

### Conclusion

A GAGG:Ce crystal scintillator contaminated with thorium was used to search for the double-alpha decay of 224Ra, setting a lower limit on its half-life.

### Motivation

Double-alpha decay is a rare nuclear decay mode that has been rarely searched for experimentally, and nuclides like 224Ra offer a possible test case given the fast decay of the daughter nucleus.

### Method

A thorium-contaminated GAGG:Ce scintillator was operated as a detector, and a time-amplitude analysis was applied to search for the characteristic two-alpha coincidence events, exploiting the 0.144 s half-life of the daughter 220Rn.

### Result

### Conclusion

The result constrains the double-alpha decay probability of 224Ra, contributing to the broader experimental study of rare nuclear decay modes.

- **Tags**: `granular flow`  `silo discharge`  `stick-slip waves`  `cohesion`  `Beverloo law`

### TL;DR

Tracking individual grains in a quasi-2D silo reveals collective deceleration waves propagating upward through the packing during discharge, and the authors examine how cohesion influences these stick-slip waves.

### Motivation

Discharge of granular material from silos follows the empirical Beverloo law with a steady outlet flow, yet the internal grain-scale dynamics that produce collective, wave-like behavior remain poorly understood, particularly when intergranular cohesion is present.

### Method

Individual grains are tracked experimentally during discharge of a quasi-2D silo, with instantaneous accelerations measured to identify collective deceleration waves, and cohesion is varied to probe its influence on wave characteristics.

### Result

Collective deceleration waves propagate upward through the packing even though the outlet flow rate stays constant and obeys the Beverloo law, with cohesion modifying the waves' characteristic properties.

### Conclusion

Stick-slip wave phenomena in silo discharge have a grain-scale origin, and cohesion plays a controlling role in their dynamics, linking microscopic grain interactions to macroscopic flow behavior.

*Keywords: granular flow, silo discharge, stick-slip waves, cohesion, Beverloo law*

### Method

### TL;DR

This study characterizes CD8+ regulatory T cells in Hantaan virus infection, showing they are diminished and terminally differentiated in hemorrhagic fever with renal syndrome patients and that they limit immunopathology while impairing viral clearance.

### Motivation

CD8+ regulatory T cells (Tregs) had not been well studied in Hantaan virus (HTNV) infection, leaving a gap in understanding how these cells shape antiviral immunity and disease severity in hemorrhagic fever with renal syndrome.

### Method

The authors examined CD8+ Treg frequencies and phenotypes in HTNV-infected patients and used infection models to assess how these cells influence immunopathology and viral control.

### Result

CD8+ Tregs were reduced and terminally differentiated in patients; their presence suppressed excessive immune pathology but also delayed viral clearance.

### Conclusion

CD8+ Tregs play a dual role in HTNV infection, restraining immunopathology at the cost of impaired viral elimination, which has implications for immunomodulatory treatment strategies.

*Keywords: CD8+ regulatory T cells, Hantaan virus, hemorrhagic fever with renal syndrome, immunopathology, viral clearance*

Climate change is intensifying extreme heat while demographic change (notably urbanization) is redistributing populations, but how these two trends jointly shape children's heat exposure had not been quantified.

The authors investigate pericyte contraction and its coupling to integrin-dependent mechanical force transmission and TGFβ activation in endothelial co-culture/vascular models.

A 22-year individual-based study of Scopoli's Shearwaters showing how environmental forcing, individual repeatability, and mother–daughter resemblance jointly shape breeding phenology in a long-lived seabird.

### Motivation

How extrinsic environmental drivers and intrinsic individual and intergenerational factors interact to shape phenological responses to environmental change in long-lived species remains poorly understood.

### Method

Longitudinal analysis of 22 years of individually marked Scopoli's Shearwaters, quantifying repeatability of phenology, mother–daughter resemblance, and responses to environmental covariates.

### Result

Phenology is shaped jointly by environmental forcing, consistent individual-level repeatability, and intergenerational resemblance between mothers and daughters.

### Motivation

Large-scale societal disruptions such as the COVID-19 pandemic may have lasting health effects, but biological evidence linking such events to epigenetic aging is limited.

### Method

The study uses prospective longitudinal data spanning childhood to adulthood with prepandemic baseline measurements to assess epigenetic aging during the pandemic.

### Result

The abstract reports evidence that pandemic-related disruption is associated with biological alterations measured through epigenetic aging, though specific effect sizes are not provided in the truncated abstract.

### Conclusion

The findings suggest that the COVID-19 pandemic may have measurable biological embedding via epigenetic aging, highlighting the value of longitudinal prepandemic baselines.

*Keywords: epigenetic aging, COVID-19 pandemic, longitudinal study, DNA methylation, biological aging*

- **Tags**: `KRAS G12C`  `tricomplex inhibitor`  `RO3903`  `adaptive resistance`  `target engagement`

### TL;DR

This study characterizes the rapid target engagement of the KRAS G12C tricomplex inhibitor RO3903 and shows that it overcomes adaptive resistance to drive durable tumor regression.

### Motivation

Transient target coverage allows oncogenic KRAS variants to cycle into drug-insensitive conformations, fueling adaptive resistance in precision oncology, so inhibitors with faster, more durable engagement are needed.

### Method

The authors perform a systematic comparative investigation of KRAS G12C targeting dynamics across inhibitors, using biochemical and cellular assays together with in vivo tumor models at Roche's China Innovation Center.

### Result

RO3903 achieves rapid target engagement via a tricomplex mechanism, overcomes resistance mechanisms observed with earlier G12C inhibitors, and drives durable tumor regression in preclinical models.

### Conclusion

Rapid, sustained target engagement with a tricomplex KRAS G12C inhibitor is a viable strategy to circumvent adaptive resistance and improve durability of targeted oncology therapy.

*Keywords: KRAS G12C, tricomplex inhibitor, RO3903, adaptive resistance, target engagement*

- **Tags**: `immigrant assimilation`  `surname change`  `Social Security records`  `historical demography`  `record linkage`

### TL;DR

Using Social Security application records from 1936–1970, the authors show that twentieth-century immigrants to the United States largely did not need to change their surnames to assimilate.

### Motivation

Surname change has long been assumed integral to immigrant assimilation and a barrier to longitudinal research, so the authors test this assumption with newly available administrative data.

### Method

Analysis of a newly digitized corpus of Social Security applications filed between 1936 and 1970, comparing recorded names across immigrant cohorts and lineages.

### Result

Contrary to the standard assimilation narrative, most immigrants retained their original surnames rather than anglicizing them.

### Conclusion

The findings revise sociological accounts of immigrant assimilation and open new possibilities for longitudinal record-linkage research on immigrants.

*Keywords: immigrant assimilation, surname change, Social Security records, historical demography, record linkage*

RNA phase separation is important for cellular function, but pathological aggregation can occur, so strategies to reverse solid RNA aggregates are needed.

### Method

The work investigates coacervate droplets as a physicochemical agent that mediates the transition of solid RNA aggregates into a liquid-like state.

### Result

Coacervate droplets are shown to promote or facilitate the solid-to-liquid transition of RNA aggregates, likely through interfacial and condensate interactions.

### Conclusion

The findings suggest coacervates can act as regulators of RNA aggregate material state, with implications for understanding and reversing pathological biomolecular condensation.

### TL;DR

Intratumoral cDC1–T cell clusters provide local costimulation that enhances cytotoxic T lymphocyte-mediated tumor rejection.

### Motivation

Effective antitumor immunity may require not only T cell infiltration but also recruitment and spatial organization of supportive cDC1 dendritic cells.

### Method

The study analyzes tumor tissue to identify cDC1–T cell clusters and assesses their role in local costimulation and CTL-mediated tumor rejection.

### Result

Tumor rejection depends on both T cell infiltration and the spatial organization of cDC1s into intratumoral clusters that support T cell costimulation.

Spatially organized cDC1–T cell interactions are critical for effective antitumor immunity and may inform cancer immunotherapy strategies.

- **Tags**: `income inequality`  `redistribution`  `cross-country comparison`  `disposable income`  `tax-benefit policy`

### TL;DR

An economics study analyzing the evolution of disposable income inequality across advanced North American and European countries from 1995 to 2021.

### Motivation

The authors aim to understand why disposable income inequality converged across transatlantic countries and rose on average, despite differing trends in market income inequality.

### Method

A cross-country comparative analysis drawing on harmonized household income data from numerous national statistical sources and research institutions across Europe and North America.

### Result

Disposable income inequality converged across advanced economies over 1995–2021 while its average level increased, a pattern driven partly by redistribution offsetting divergent market-income trends.

### Conclusion

Redistribution and tax-transfer policies play a central role in shaping cross-country inequality dynamics and explain the observed transatlantic convergence.

*Keywords: income inequality, redistribution, cross-country comparison, disposable income, tax-benefit policy*

The molecular mechanisms governing mammalian fertilization, particularly acrosome anchoring and perinuclear theca formation, remain poorly understood.

### TL;DR

Behavioral experiments in spiders provide evidence for a two-stage sleep-like state, suggesting REM-like and non-REM-like phases may predate vertebrates.

### Motivation

Sleep research is dominated by vertebrate and few model-species data, leaving the evolutionary origins and functions of sleep poorly understood.

### Method

Behavioral monitoring of spiders (likely using motion/body-posture tracking and response-threshold assays) to identify sleep-like states and signs of two distinct sleep stages.

### Result

Spiders show behavioral signatures consistent with two-stage sleep, including period-specific reductions in responsiveness and stage-dependent postural or behavioral features.

### Conclusion

Two-stage sleep may be evolutionarily older and more widespread than previously assumed, extending sleep-stage biology beyond vertebrates.

*Keywords: sleep stages, REM-like sleep, comparative sleep, arachnid behavior, evolution of sleep*

- **Tags**: `lncRNA Synage`  `sperm quality`  `A-type potassium currents`  `GnRH neurons`  `reproductive aging`

### TL;DR

The paper identifies a hypothalamic long noncoding RNA, Synage, that protects against age-related sperm quality decline by suppressing A-type potassium currents in GnRH neurons.

### Motivation

Paternal aging reduces sperm quality and increases health risks to offspring, but the underlying molecular and neural mechanisms remain incompletely understood.

### Method

The authors investigate the role of the lncRNA Synage in the hypothalamus, likely using knockout/knockdown models, electrophysiology, and fertility assays to link Synage to potassium currents in GnRH neurons.

### Result

Synage is shown to prevent age-related sperm quality decline, acting by suppressing A-type potassium currents in GnRH neurons.

### Conclusion

The findings reveal a hypothalamic lncRNA-based mechanism controlling reproductive aging and suggest Synage as a potential target for mitigating paternal age-related fertility decline.

*Keywords: lncRNA Synage, sperm quality, A-type potassium currents, GnRH neurons, reproductive aging*
